Like, I didn't think there was going to be an ambulance crash or a fire or something like that. It wasn't a specific event I could name. \n\nIt was just... this overwhelming feeling that things would go bad. The whole bay would just be wrong, or tainted somehow. I don't really know how else to describe it, I just didn't know exactly what would happen. I just knew I couldn't stop or else it would.\n\nDoctor: Did you think that not finishing the pattern would actually cause something bad to happen in a way that was outside of your control or unrelated to your actions at the station?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said, I just felt like the bay itself would be wrong or ruined if I didn't finish it. Like, I didn't think there was going to be an ambulance crash or a fire or something like that. It wasn't a specific event I could name. \n\nIt was just... this overwhelming feeling that things would go bad. The whole bay would just be wrong, or tainted somehow. I don't really know how else to describe it, I just didn't know exactly what would happen. I just knew I couldn't stop or else it would.\n\nDoctor: Did you think that not finishing the pattern would actually cause something bad to happen in a way that was outside of your control or unrelated to your actions at the station?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I didn't think about it like... like it was going to cause some random disaster somewhere else or something totally outside of my control. It was just right there in front of me. \n\nIt felt completely tied to what I was doing with the bags at that exact moment. Like I said, I just felt like the bay itself would be wrong or ruined if I didn't finish it. I didn't really think about it connecting to anything else outside of that.\n\nDoctor: Did you think that your belief about something bad happening if you didn't finish the pattern was definitely true, even if others didn't understand why?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I really... I didn't think about it in terms of it being a definite fact, or whether the other guys understood it or not. I wasn't really focused on them at all. \n\nIt was just... I just had that overwhelming feeling that I needed to finish it. I didn't really stop to debate if it was true or if my lieutenant got what I was doing. I just knew I had to do it right then, so I didn't really think about the rest of it.\n\nDoctor: Did you think that your belief about something bad happening if you didn't finish the pattern was realistic?\n\nPatient: I don't think I ever really stopped to consider if it was realistic or not. Um... when you're in the middle of it, you just feel this intense pressure. It wasn't about logic or being realistic. I just... I just felt like I had to do it, so I did. I wasn't really analyzing it at the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So, yes, I honestly believed it.\n\nDoctor: Did you think that not finishing the pattern would actually cause something bad to happen, like a real event?\n\nPatient: I didn't really have a specific picture in my head of what it would be. It wasn't a specific event I could name. \n\nIt was just... this overwhelming feeling that things would go bad. The whole bay would just be wrong, or tainted somehow.
